# Task status

The running record of the cards in [`TASKS.md`](TASKS.md). Updated at every merge. Branch
`plan/task-cards` holds all merged work; `main` holds the work up to pull request 2. Each card was done
by an agent on its own branch `task/<ID>`, audited by the orchestrating session, and merged after its
tests passed on the merged tree.

**6m. `C5` is built; its check reported a failure that is the check's own error; the release was started on a
signal that should not have been given. Read this before trusting "all passed" (23:00 CST, 2026-11-20).**
- **What ran**: `c47-c5-2` ended 21:49:10 with exit 0 (48 run records, 8,899.7 CPU-min, largest process 232.8;
  the 42 judged steps at 0.758 to 1.204 of their estimates: the 1.204 is the ingest record DEC-56 accepts, the 0.758
  the near-Cas CRISPR step, recorded and not stopped, DEC-53; unit `MemoryPeak` 15.1 GB). `after` (22:01): all seven
  releases verified, manifest hashes as before. `backup` (22:05): `added_c5.tar`, 5,821,644,800 bytes, sha256
  `421a3bbde54b8b46c613a1007a969403851d6b3922b3c4844b8324a5ddfe12ae`, on `/stor/work`. Stage `C5`: 2,029 genomes
  called, 2,653 contigs stored (9,109,234,757 bp), 8,476,374 genes, 3,972,512 distinct proteins (3,285,916 in no
  earlier stage), 1,761 default and 87 filtered CRISPR arrays; 138 files, 5,821,546,544 bytes (*measured*,
  `/tmp/if2453/c47/check/summary.json`, run records, driver log).
- **The failure**: `c47_check.py` ended `RESULT: FAILED: genecall ledger earlier skips (2553) != stored_by (2550)`
  and exited 1 (`driver.log`, 22:02:41). It was the only failed assertion.
- **Why the queue went on anyway (a bug in `c47_driver.sh`)**: its `check` function returns the check's status, but
  the script's last command is a log line, so `c47_driver.sh check` exits 0. `c47_queue.sh` saw exit 0, ran
  `backup`, and wrote "done: ... all passed"; `relwait-1120` took that line at its word and started the release
  train at 22:06. The orchestrator read `c47_driver.sh` in the morning and missed this. Every other mode fails
  through `fail`, which exits 1; only `check` is affected. (`c48c_v1_driver.sh` was written in the same form: its
  check printed `RESULT: all checks pass`, read by the orchestrator in the file itself, so `V1` is not in doubt, but
  the same bug is to be looked for there.)
- **What the failure is (read by the orchestrator itself, read-only, `/tmp/if2453/audit1120/c5_skips.py`;
  *measured*, wilkcomp02, 22:53)**: the gene-call ledger has 5,206 rows: 2,653 `ok`; 2,550 `owned_by:<stage>` for
  `PL1` 2,543, `P2` 3, `P4` 2, `P1` 1, `S1` 1, which is exactly the check's own count of contigs stored by earlier
  stages, stage by stage; and **3 rows `owned_by:C5/<genome_uid>`**: contigs that two genomes of `C5` both list,
  called once under the genome of least `genome_uid` (DEC-27). The check drops within-stage rows only when the
  reason is exactly `owned_by:C5`; the code writes `owned_by:C5/<genome_uid>`, so it counted those 3 as skips for an
  earlier stage. The three contigs (`567c66dd…`, `a10259a7…`, `a43f9ae9…`; 41,190, 2,711 and 43,273 bp) are stored by
  `C5` and by no other stage, and each has genes in `C5`'s `feature_protein`; all 2,653 stored contigs have genes;
  no contig is stored by `C5` and another stage; no skipped contig is unheld. **So the stage is as it should be and
  the check is wrong.** The fixture and the 20-genome rehearsal have no contig shared within the stage, which is why
  the rehearsal passed.
- **Decision, the orchestrator's, for the PI to overrule**: the release train is left running (`r26.11.2` over
  `S1,P1..P6,H1,PL1,C5,V1`; at `cluster.nr90.06` at 22:54). Nothing in it depends on the check script; nothing is
  announced until: card C-47's agent has fixed the check (test first: a within-stage row) and the driver's exit
  status, the corrected `check` has passed on the real root (read-only), and the orchestrator has audited the
  release. To stop the release instead: a `STOP` file in the data root (`touch /tmp/if2453/r10/root/STOP`).
- **The corrected check passed** (23:03:28, unit `c47-check2`, `/tmp/if2453/c47/check2/check.out`, last line
  `RESULT: all checks pass`, read by the orchestrator at 23:04): `ledger_skipped_earlier` 2,550, equal to
  `stored_by` stage by stage; `ledger_skipped_within` 3; a new assertion, every stored contig has genes or a ledger
  reason: 2,653 of 2,653 with genes. It compared the 17:18 `before` with the 21:51 `after` snapshot, so the
  release's new files do not enter. `root_added.tsv` of both checks and the driver's copy have one sha256
  (`901be1bd…4ec7`; 138 files, 5,821,546,544 bytes), and the tar on `/stor/work` lists the same 138 members. The
  first `check/` is kept as the record of the false failure. The fix (commit `25ac362` on `task/C-47`; copies in
  `/tmp/if2453/c47/bin`): the check compares the owner's stage, not the reason string; the driver exits with its
  mode's status; `check` and `backup` write `DONE` only on success; the queue requires each mode's marker. **So the
  condition the release was started on does hold, after the fact.**
- **A point of stage order, judged harmless by the agent and the orchestrator**: `C5`'s scan plan copied the Pfam
  rows of 16,645 proteins from `V1` (card B-47b), because `V1` was published before `C5`'s scan although `C5`'s
  inputs were recorded first, and a release lists `C5` before `V1`. The copied rows are measured rows of the same
  proteins under the same library, checked row for row against `V1`'s, and they are written into `C5`'s own files:
  a release that had `C5` without `V1` would still read them. Noted for whoever next touches DEC-35 (8).
- **`c48c_v1_driver.sh`** does not have the check bug (its `check` goes through `fail`), but has the same final
  log line after its `case`, so a mode that returned a failure without `fail` would be lost, and it ignores the

file and in cards C-24 and C-26, will be `r26.11.1`** (nothing in the root carries the old name). Releases are
rolling: one is cut whenever a stage has been completed since the last release and no release is being built,
over every complete stage in the order built (card C-46 makes the driver; card W-37 adds `--release latest`).
The built releases keep their names; `rS<k>.<n>` stays reserved for a signed release at a plan stage's gate.

  2. `scripts/release_examples.sh rM.2` (RUNBOOK §6): **run, and it stopped the announcement** (*measured*,
     wilkcomp02, 2026-11-14, 22:13 to 22:15, unit `ex-rM2`, tree `/tmp/if2453/ci/rM2post` at `ac24001`, output
     `/tmp/if2453/ex-rM2/out`, log `/tmp/if2453/ex-rM2/examples.log`): "rM.2: 2 examples, 37 invariant(s) failed or
     missing (crispr_cas_neighbourhoods: 19, rnap: 18)". Both examples stop at their first step, finding the
     positive control by accession: `bio-box find` refuses with "no contig of release rM.2 has the accession
     NC_001604" (phage T7, stage S1) and "... NZ_AP026781.1" (stage P2), though both contigs are in the release.
     Cause (*measured*, `ls`): `l0seq/contig_label/` has files for P3 to P6 only; S1, P1 and P2 were built before
     card B-27, and the fallback of card W-18 applies only to a release with no label file at all. So in `rM.2`
     the contigs of S1, P1 and P2 (32,815 of 54,265, *derived*) have no accession for any lookup, and neither
     `release verify` nor `bio-box releases` says so. No search was run; the data and the pack are not in doubt.
     **Card W-36** fixes the readers (per stage: labels where a stage has them, else from `contig_occurrence`),
     makes `release verify` and `releases` name the unlabelled stages, and reruns the release check on `rM.2`.
     When every invariant holds: its note into `planning/measurements/`, each example's `recorded/rM.2.tsv`, then
     the announcement of `rM.2` in `README.md` and `GUIDE.md`. **Until then `rN.3` stays the release to use.**
     **Fixed and passed** (card W-36, merged 2026-11-15): with the readers fixed, the check ran again from 22:38
     to 00:06 (unit `w36-ex-rM2`, output `/tmp/if2453/w36/ex-rM2`): "rM.2: 2 examples, every invariant holds", 37
     invariants ok, 55 recorded values new (*measured*; the orchestrator read `summary.tsv` and ran both lookups

*Standing facts a new session needs.*
- Worked examples are release checks (AGENTS §5.7): `bash "$TREE/scripts/release_examples.sh" <release>` on the pod
  with RUNBOOK §0's environment and a new `OUT`; both examples pass on `rN.3` (92 checks).
- `README.md` holds the scope and the milestones: keep its "Now" and "Done" lists current when a milestone lands.
- Helpers: `scripts/orchestrator/wait_run.sh <repo> <run id>` (polls an Actions run to completion; `gh run watch`
  returns early); `watch_until.sh`, `watch_ci.sh` (below).

**For a new session.**
1. `git fetch origin`. The integration worktree is `.claude/worktrees/integration` in the main checkout, on local
   branch `integration`, which tracks `origin/plan/task-cards`: merge there and push with
   `git push origin HEAD:plan/task-cards`. The main checkout itself is on an old branch; do not merge there.
2. Agents outlive a restart of the session: list the live agents and look for a running `pod_pytest.sh <ID>` before
   restarting any card (a second agent once started in a live card's worktree).
3. Watch pod jobs with `scripts/orchestrator/watch_until.sh '<remote test>' '<remote report>' [interval]` (one short
   ssh call per interval; long-held ssh sessions drop with exit 255). For a full suite that must survive drops:
   `pod_pytest.sh <name> tests/r0/test_runbook.py` to sync, then on the pod `bash
   /tmp/if2453/ci/<name>/scripts/orchestrator/detached_suite.sh <name> 2` (the synced tree's own copy; the copy at
   `/tmp/if2453/ci-tmp/detached_suite.sh` was stale until 20:15 on 2026-11-13 and ran F-02 session 8's suite in the
   old image), poll `systemctl --user is-active 'ci-<name>-*'` and `tail -n 1 /tmp/if2453/ci-tmp/<name>-detached/log.*`.
4. Pass `watch_ci.sh` the full sha from `git rev-parse`. `ci_repeat.sh <repo> <branch> <n>` reruns `ci` n times.
5. Since card C-29 the pod's test image has every locked package (zombi2 and matplotlib included): set no
   `BIOBOX_POD_PYDEPS`. The directories `/tmp/if2453/pilot_s1/pydeps`, `pydeps_f02` and `pydeps_w27` are needed only by
   the running 50 Gbp build's old image (`pilot_s1/pydeps`; never modify it) and can go, with the PI's word, once that
   build is done. The cross-host test needs `BIOBOX_PEER_MANIFEST` naming a file with the Actions `cross-host` job's
   hash line. Since card C-38e (three round `TableSpec`s added to `manifest["tables"]`) the hash is `5fa9e49761393e3468ebc3c1d7d7224a`
   (it was `003ab250cfc3de20b2381ebdf7beba5c` from C-38d through C-35, and `28ffa9808ecd4829e52f6892f94f7b85` from E-12 through C-41), and `/tmp/if2453/ci-peer/right.json` holds it
   since 06:26 on 2026-11-19 (the cross-host test passes on the merged tree with it).

**How the work runs.** One card per agent, in its own worktree (`.claude/worktrees/agent-*`) on branch
`task/<ID>` (measurements: `measure/<ID>`); tests only on the pod (`sh scripts/pod_pytest.sh <ID> [paths]`). The
orchestrating session audits each report, merges `--no-ff --no-commit` into the integration worktree, runs the
affected tests on the merged tree (`env BIOBOX_TREE=<worktree> sh scripts/pod_pytest.sh integration <paths>`; a
merged tree identical to the agent's tested tree needs no rerun; **without `BIOBOX_TREE` the script tests the main
checkout, an old branch, and passes**: that happened once on 2026-11-13, see row C-29), commits, adds the card's row to the table below,
pushes, and checks the GitHub Actions run of the pushed commit. Sonnet for tier-L cards, Opus for the rest, at most
four agents at once (two heavy ones since the evening of 2026-11-13, for the usage limit). When a card conflicts with its own text, a decision or a file, the agent stops and reports;
the card is amended in `TASKS.md` and the agent resumed (B-45, C-21c, E-09 went that way today).

schema's hash, so it moves with any schema change (last: card E-12).

### Trial search on `rN.1`, 22:50 CST: a search cannot run on it yet

Record: `planning/measurements/2026-11-10_trial_search_rN1.md` (all *measured*, wilkcomp02, read-only root).
- Current code opens and verifies `rN.1` (60 s), finds T7 by accession, and exports all 10.6 M proteins (88 s).
- **Blocker:** `pack build` holds every region in memory; it hit a 32 GiB cap in the dark-ORF part and
  thrashed, so no pack exists and `search run` refuses. Card W-16.
- **Wrong answer:** `nbr summarize --by nr50` exits 0 with every neighbour `unannotated`, because `rN.1`
  predates `member_cluster` (B-27). Card W-17. The rebuild's releases need the newer tables: card C-17.
- The answer by hand (`hmmsearch` on the export, Pfam `RNA_pol`, 30 bits): 1,957 proteins, 1,931 in phages
  (equal to the 2026-11-09 walk-through) and 26 in prokaryotes; T7 gp1 scores 367.7 bits.
- Ten more rough edges: cards W-18 and W-15.
- Night threads: the PI raised the scan to 12 shards (48 threads) from the P2 stage, leaving 8 for tests;
  driver unit `r10-p2-driver2` replaced `r10-p2-driver` at 22:09 CST.

`scripts/orchestrator/`.

## Merged (148 cards, and measurements; F-02 has ten sessions in; C-48c has parts 2 and 3, and C-48e part 1, the inputs, in)

| Card | Merge | What it did | Audit notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-13 | this merge | **The release train can carry a record accepted by a decision row, and carries none.** `train_est.py` gets a table `ACCEPTED`, empty: an entry names one release exactly, a step pattern (`fnmatchcase` on the whole step name), the greatest ratio accepted (over 1.20, at most 1.60: DEC-55's, the largest the PI has accepted) and its decision row. A judged step over 1.20 that an entry matches at or under its bound is listed under `accepted_over_20pct` with the row's name, stays in `outside_20pct`, and does not stop the train; above the bound it stops as now, its row naming the entry. Decisions DEC-18 to DEC-23 were approved by the PI on 2026-11-09 and are in `planning/DECISIONS.md`.

## CI was not reliably green (found 2026-11-10; resolved by A-07, merged 2026-11-10)

GitHub Actions `ci` failed on 7 of the last 12 pushes to `plan/task-cards` while the pod suite passed on
the same commits: two search tests fail intermittently there (card A-07). The orchestrating session
checked CI only after the first push and missed this for a day. The merge to `main` waits for A-07.
The `gh` token has `read:packages` since 2026-11-10, so the image can be pulled once `main` builds it.

## Plan for larger imports (approved by the PI, 2026-11-10)

1. When card C-10 reports a clean real run of `build-stage`: rebuild the 10 Gbp set (the phages and
   2,497 prokaryotes of `rL.3`) with the current code, under the night cap, on the pod's local disk,
   then sync to the provisional area on `/stor`. It replaces `rL.3`, which current code cannot open.
2. When card B-24 merges: build all 11,973 complete GTDB species representatives (about 50 Gbp,
   *derived*: 4.25 Mbp per genome from the 2,497 already fetched) as several stages of about 10 Gbp.
   Expected wall time about 2.5 days, of which Pfam about 1.5 (*derived* from rates in
   `planning/measurements/2026-11-10_import_ladder/`).
The thread cap is the sum over all our processes: 40 by day, 56 at night.

## Not yet executed anywhere

These exist and pass their laptop-runnable parts, but their real run needs the image:
the clustering tests; the samtools slice and index tests; the HMMER-binary oracles (two tests);
the golden pairs test over postings; the Prodigal test (also needs `prodigal=2.6.3` added to
`container/environment.yml`, a PI decision); the mutation workflow; the systemd path of `run-step`
and of the search worker (only their command lines are unit-tested; the scope behaviour itself was
probed by hand on the pod).

15. **Decided by the PI on 2026-11-20 at about 09:28 CST: "yes, accept c5 ingest and resume" (DEC-56).** Card C-47's

   the root). The question as it stood: **Stage `C5`'s ingest cost 20.35% more than its estimate: accept that one record, or not?** The launch `c47-c5-1`
   stopped on it (45.803 CPU-minutes against 38.057, ratio 1.204; *measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-19,

   FASTA; card B-58 adds the term). Accepting it means one line in `c47_est.py` in the form of DEC-47's
   (`ACCEPTED = {("C5", "ingest", 45.803, 38.057): ...}`) and a DEC row; the driver then resumes at gene calling.
   **The orchestrator may not lift a guard by itself** (it tried on 2026-11-20 on its own reading of "continue to
   run ingests" and was stopped by the session's permission check; the edit was undone). Recommendation: accept.
   Until then `C5` stays ingested and incomplete, cards C-47, B-58, B-47a to B-47e and stages `C1` to `C4` and `C6`
   wait, and the release train leaves `C5` out of every release (it names it and does not wait).
16. **Card B-56 cannot be done as written; it needs a design decision.** The agent's reading is in the card: the
   gene caller resolves keys on two unrelated sort orders, so ordering genomes one way leaves three lookups random.
   Choices: (a) a sorted merge join for the stage (changes the batch order the gene-call plan is defined on, and
   touches `inputs.py` and `store.py`); (b) a per-batch sorted prefetch, about 2× and not O(1); (c) leave it and
   size memory for it. **Corrected 2026-11-20: it does not hold `PL2`.** The root reaches about 2.4 M contigs with
   `V1`, `C1` to `C6`, `PL2` and `G1` together (*derived*, card B-56); the decision is needed before the IMG-UMAG
   prokaryote stages (`J2` on) or GlobDB's `G2` on, which are also held for other reasons.

19. **Where the corpus lives once it passes the pod's local disk** (opened 2026-11-20, after DEC-57 and DEC-58).
   The data root is on `/tmp` (1.1 TB in all, 613 GB free, shared, cleared at the reboot of 2026-11-17; *measured*,
   `df`, 2026-11-20 10:22), with its durable copy as tars on `/stor/work` (68 TB, 19 TB free, the group's, backed
   up). We hold **1.2 TB on `/stor/work`** (*measured*, `du`), almost all of it the JGI archives, where the footprint
   draft (`planning/footprint_request.md`) says "< 50 GB" there and 1.5 to 2 TB on `/stor/scratch`; whether that draft
   was ever sent or agreed is not recorded. SPIRE's contigs of 5 kb and more are about 3.76 Tbp (*derived*, card
   C-42), about 3 TB at depth `called` (*derived*: 0.85 GB per Gbp, *measured* at 1.5 kb, the pilot), which no
   placement on `/tmp` holds. Choices: (a) tranches on the pod until `/tmp` is at its floor (two or three of 50 to
   100 Gbp), then stop; (b) the root, or its older releases and stages, on `/stor` with the pod owner's agreement to
   a stated footprint; (c) TACC (card C-37, waiting on the PI's two TxRAS requests, card C-37a). Recommendation:
   (a) now, (c) for the body of SPIRE, and tell the pod owner what is on `/stor/work` today. Until this is answered
   no fetch is started whose result cannot be removed again without deleting under `/stor`.
   **What bio-box holds on `/stor`, measured 2026-11-20 (`du`, wilkcomp02)**: 1.2 TB under `/stor/work/Wilke/if2453`
   (the JGI archives, 1.14 TB, and the root's tars, 107 GB) and **164 GB under
   `/stor/scratch/Wilke/if2453/all-the-data`** (163 GB of it `provisional/`: stage inputs and older roots; 1.1 GB the
   reference libraries). The account's other 6.5 TB on `/stor/scratch` are the PI's other projects, not this one.
   Against the footprint draft: scratch is far under its 1.5 to 2 TB; `/stor/work` is 24 times over its "< 50 GB".
   **The PI at about 10:55 on 2026-11-20 (DEC-60 (3))**: agrees this is the main constraint, and offers a fourth
   place, (d) the PI's NAS `tower` ("as we grow, maybe tranches can move there?"): 32 TB array with 18 TB free, 8
   cores, 31 GB of memory (*measured* from the laptop, 2026-11-20), on the PI's home network, which the pod cannot
   reach. It can hold tranches and source archives; it cannot build, cluster or serve searches at the pod's rate.
   Card C-49 measures the routes and proposes tiers; **nothing is copied there before its report and the PI's word
   on the route** (a key for `tower` to pull from the pod is the PI's to make). Item 19 stays open until then.
   **Card C-49's report (merged 2026-11-20; `planning/design/C-49_tiers.md`)**: `tower` works as the second durable
   copy and as a home for source archives, staged inputs and the deferred-data lists. **It cannot hold tranches of
   built stages**: the builders find earlier stages by what is on the pod's disk, and a release must verify every
   file it lists, so anything a release uses stays on the machine that computes. Where the corpus lives once it
   passes the pod's local disk is therefore still (b) `/stor` with an agreed footprint or (c) TACC; `tower` takes
   the pressure off `/stor/work` (1.2 TB of ours there, 32.6 hours of relay for the JGI archives) but not off `/tmp`.
   The note also offers a way to end the `/stor/work` overage with no relay: the JGI archives could sit on
   `/stor/scratch`, where 1.5 to 2 TB were asked for and 164 GB are used (the PI's, with the pod owner: nothing under
   `/stor` is moved by an agent). **For the PI, from the note's section 7**: retention on `tower`; when a `/stor` copy
   may go; a second copy of `tower`; and DEC-52's open point, how many old releases keep their large files.

20. **How a killed worker's memory peak is recorded** (card A-10, diagnosed 2026-11-20; a design choice, tier H).
   One Actions job in 31 recorded a sampled peak 37% under the cap for an OOM-killed search, because the helper
   that reads the final `memory.peak` was itself killed with the worker. It cannot be fixed by ordering inside the
   helper. It touches no published run record (those are written only on exit 0; 0 of 1,908 in the root are
   sampled, *measured*); it touches records of failed steps and of searches that ended `oom`, `timeout` or killed,
   and it makes `ci` red about once in 31 jobs. Choices (the card has them): (a) a keeper process or a helper
   outside the scope; (b) systemd's own accounting; (c) a sampler that charges nothing to the scope.
   Recommendation: decide after card A-10a's evidence says who kills the helper; until then a red `ci` on that one
   test alone is run again once and noted in the push's record, and `plan/task-cards` is not merged into `main`.

   threads).

   `task/C-47` until its merge; *measured*, `breakdown.json`, whose rule-as-built reproduces `eval.json`'s totals):**

   **The orchestrator's recommendation, for the PI to take or not**: (d) for the stages on hand. Scan `C1` to `C4`

**Open since 13:55 on 2026-11-16** (F-02 session 13; its note is `planning/measurements/2026-11-14_synthworld_redesign.md`
§16 on branch `task/F-02-13`, at `f340a0a`, pushed, not merged):

12. **The positive control at the null module's second seed.** Session 13 found DEC-48 (1)'s premise false
   (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-16, 22-seed scans): with partners at the top level the partner is in nearly
   every genome (share 0.96 to 0.99 against the decoy test's 0.9), and at low rates decoys die before 3 transfers
   wherever the partners sit; a rate of 4.0 or more is needed, and at every rate that holds at all 22 seeds decoys
   are 23 to 48 genes per genome. It took the fallback: middle-level partners at rate 6.0 (46.9 decoy genes per
   genome at the default seed) and the KS test judged over 22 seeds (at most 2 failing; measured 1 and 0).
   DEC-48 (2) is done (one truth row per `feature_uid`). What fails now: `test_n0_has_no_planted_signal` at seed
   20261113, in its positive control on the signal world: 2 of 75 planted pairs exceed tau on flank contexts, p
   0.0025 where the test asks below 1e-6 (session 12's world had 7 of 75 there). Options: (a) one more draw
   (another partner set at rate 7.0), checked again over 22 seeds; (b) judge the positive control over the 22
   seeds, as the KS test now is; (c) run the module's second seed at another seed. **Recommendation: (b)**: (a)
   and (c) search for a draw that passes, and this is the third check found to hang on one seed's draw (after
   the IGR pair count and the level step). Also for the PI: decoys near single copy cannot be had while every
   decoy must have 3 transfers at every seed; either they stay a family of 30 to 50 genes, or that rule is
   relaxed. A permission check of the session refused the agent's second scan unit on the pod; it stopped, and
   nothing was worked around.

**Decided by the PI on 2026-11-17 at about 21:22** (`"13, y ; 14 confirm"`, in answer to the orchestrator's summary of

estimate; steps >20% under estimate are logged/reported and get a refit card without stopping the build; unblocks
release `r26.11.1`, card C-35) and DEC-54 (item 14: all fourteen recommended answers of card C-38's import-depths
design confirmed; cards C-38a to C-38h copied into `TASKS.md` and unblocked). **Items 13 and 14 are no longer open**;
they are kept as the record of what was asked.

**Asked at 09:14 on 2026-11-17** (the design note of card C-38, `planning/design/C-38_import_depths.md` §9; each
is answerable in a word; the orchestrator's recommended answer follows each):

14. **The fourteen questions of the import-depths design.**
   (1) "Clustered" is a property of a release, so a stage's depth is stored, called or annotated: *yes*.
   (2) A release takes only stages at depth called or more: *yes*.
   (3) A release's search pack holds the intergenic regions and dark ORFs of its called stages too (invariant 16
   as written), or proteins only: *leave until card C-42 measures the pack's size per Gbp; until then as the
   invariant says*.
   (4) Genes on each side of a hit that a round covers: *10*.
   (5) Round rows are kept out of family tokens and postings and shown only in neighbour tables and exports: *yes*
   (they cluster around earlier hits and would bias counts).
   (6) A release takes every round complete when it is cut, and no release is cut for rounds alone: *yes*.
   (7) Rounds run only as steps of the build account, never inside a user's search: *yes*.
   (8) An annotated stage scans the proteins of contigs that a called stage owns in its genomes: *yes*.
   (9) After card C-38c, one clustering method for the whole release line, chosen then: *yes*.
   (10) When a release's proteins cannot be clustered at once on any machine we have, DEC-24 stands (no such
   release) or is revisited: *stands for now; revisit only with card C-38c's and card C-37's numbers in hand*.
   (11) A contig dropped by the length filter is outside the corpus, so invariant 2 of the spec does not cover
   it: *yes*.
   (12) The least contig length: *after card C-42's measured shares*.
   (13) Filter by length only, not by gene count: *yes* (length is known before gene calling).
   (14) Pfam on representatives of large NR50 clusters as rounds, members shown "by cluster" and never written as
   inferred rows: *leave until card C-38c*.
   Cards C-38a (depth), C-38b (domain state) and C-38d (the filter's mechanism) can start once (1), (2), (11) and
   (13) are answered.

**Asked at 23:35 on 2026-11-16** (the release `r26.11.1`, card C-35; *measured*, wilkcomp02,
`/tmp/if2453/c35/driver.log`):

13. **How the release build resumes, and whether a step that costs less than its estimate should stop a build at
   all.** The launch `c35-rel-1` began at 17:44 on 2026-11-16. By 23:34 it had 143 run records: the plan, every
   NR90 clustering step and the NR90 representatives, 8,023 CPU-minutes against 8,167 estimated for those steps
   (the largest part at 1.02 of its estimate; scratch peak 68 GB). Then the first NR50 step,
   `cluster.nr50.00.kmermatcher`, cost 19.3 CPU-minutes against an estimate of 31.4 (0.615; estimated above
   DEC-43's 24, so judged), and the card's rate guard stopped the unit at 23:34:57. Nothing is wrong in the root;
   the same command resumes at the next NR50 step, but the guard reads that record again and would stop at once.
   This is the third stop of a build by a guard, and the second for a step that came in cheaper (DEC-47 was the
   first). Options: (a) accept this one record by name and value, as DEC-47 did, and resume; (b) the same, and
   change the rule for every build driver: **a judged step more than 20% over its estimate stops the build; a
   step more than 20% under is reported in the note and gets a card to correct the estimate, and does not stop
   it**; (c) leave the release until the PI has looked. **Recommendation: (b)**: a step under its estimate
   threatens no budget (the 300 CPU-minute limit, memory, disk), and each such stop has cost a night. AGENTS §6
   would then read as it does for documents and cards, with the build guards judging over-runs only. Not done
   by the orchestrator: it changes what a stop condition judges (DEC-43, DEC-47 were the PI's).

**Asked at 21:33 on 2026-11-15** (the build of stage `PL1`, card C-26 part 2):

11. **How the `PL1` build resumes.** Its rate guard stopped it after ingest: 26.612 CPU-minutes against the code's
   estimate of 33.267 (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-15, the step's run record), 20.005% under, and AGENTS §6
   says stop above 20%. Nothing is wrong with what was written (61,647 new sequences of 72,556 records; the six
   releases verify). A likely cause, not yet shown from the records: the estimate is made from the FASTA bytes
   read, and 16.6% of `PL1`'s bases are contigs the root already stores and are not written again (*derived*:
   1 − 5,924,552,875 / 7,102,391,741). The same command resumes at gene calling, but the guard reads the ingest
   record again at every check, so it would stop again at once. Options: (a) resume with this one ingest record
   accepted by name and value in the driver, every later step still judged at 20%; (b) first correct the ingest
   estimate in the code (a card: the estimate from the bases to be stored, fitted on the built stages), then
   resume with the guard unchanged; (c) leave `PL1` until you have looked. **Recommendation: (a), and (b) as a
   follow-up card that does not hold the build**: the step came in cheaper, by 0.005 of a percentage point
   past the line, and no process budget depends on it. I did not do (a) myself because it changes what a stop
   condition judges, which you decided yourself for H1 (DEC-43). The cap is 40 threads by day on weekdays, so a
   resume by day runs `PL1` at 36 with at most 4 for everything else of ours.

**Asked at 18:40 on 2026-11-15** (F-02 session 12; its note is `planning/measurements/2026-11-14_synthworld_redesign.md`
§15 on branch `task/F-02-12`). They hold the statistics track only; builds and the other cards go on.

9. **Decoys against the N0 test** (DEC-44 (2) said: raise decoy transfer until every decoy has 3 transfers at every
   seed). *Measured* over 22 seeds (wilkcomp02, 2026-11-15): every decoy reaches 3 transfers at 9, 15, 15, 21, 22
   seeds at rates 0.4, 0.8, 1.2, 4.0, 8.0. At 8.0 the test that N0 keeps root-family presence in distribution (a KS
   test of the null world against the signal world) fails at 3 of 22 seeds for N0-full and 4 for N0-strict, the null
   module's two seeds among them (D 0.0917 against 0.0728; 0.0785 against 0.0736); at 0.4 it held at all 22. At
   12.0 the KS holds at both module seeds and decoys miss at 2 seeds; at 16.0 both hold and the default world has
   193 cross-phylum IGR pairs, under DEC-44 (5)'s 200. At 8.0 decoys are also multi-copy: 40.8 genes per genome
   against 2.2 before. Options: (a) keep 8.0 and judge the KS over the 22 seeds (a count of failing seeds that its
   significance level allows) instead of at two seeds; (b) accept a rate that misses some seeds (4.0: 21 of 22, the
   fewest 1 transfer); (c) move the decoy partners to modules with higher transfer rates (they sit at the two lowest
   levels, and a decoy fails when its partner is soon held by one lineage only), so that a low decoy rate lands 3
   times, and leave the tests as they are. **Recommendation: (c)**, one session, with (a) as the fallback if (c)
   does not hold at all 22 seeds: 8.0 buys the 3 transfers by making decoys a 40-gene family in every genome, which
   is a different object from the decoy the design describes, and (c) removes the cause instead.
10. **Two identical contigs in one genome** (genome g036: a young exact tandem triplication breaks the assembly into
   two contigs of 1,427 bp with the same sequence, so one `contig_uid`, and two truth genes at the same coordinates
   share a `feature_uid`; the test that each `feature_uid` appears once fails). DEC-27 covers identical contigs
   across genomes, not within one. Options: (a) the truth holds one row per `feature_uid` that names both genes, as
   the store itself holds an identical contig once with two occurrences; (b) the assembly step joins or drops
   identical contigs of a genome. **Recommendation: (a)**: real assemblies have such contigs (the PLSDB inputs had
   3,863 repeated records), so the truth should describe what the store can represent, not avoid the case.

**Decided by the PI on 2026-11-15 at about 13:15** ("1. a, 2 y, 3, yes one release, 4. yes, 5. allowed, 6., leave it, 7. keep run-wide, 8., keep both", then "at most 4 agents at once"): DEC-43 (item 7: the H1 build resumes with the guard narrowed), DEC-44 (item 5: the four tests of the synthetic world), DEC-45 (item 6: one release `rM.3` over nine stages, and a card that lets a release take an earlier clustering; item 1: `rM.1` left), DEC-46 (scratch of finished cards may be removed; item 2: the one-colour rule run-wide; the PLSDB defaults; item 4: at most four agents). Item 3 was closed by measurement (card E-16). **Nothing below is open**; the items are kept as the record of what was asked:

   library and, *measured*, the same cluster bytes as `rM.2`, so finishing it would write a second copy of `rM.2`.

4. A third heavy agent while the pod is free at the weekend cap: not taken; the limit of two stands.
5. **F-02 session 11: the synthetic world's redesign works per seed but breaks six assertions** (branch
   `task/F-02-11`, not merged; `planning/measurements/2026-11-14_synthworld_redesign.md` on that branch).
   What holds now over 22 seeds (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-14): the crown is 5.0 at every seed; the
   chromosome mean is 1,152 to 1,799, inside DEC-37's 30% at 22 of 22 (session 10: 6 of 21); the planted share is
   3.2% to 7.7% (it was 4.3% to 70.8%); no module family enters a genome that holds the module (DEC-36), at 22 of
   22; the three transfer levels of the strength grid are distinct and increase at 22 of 22 (medians 5.0, 10.6 and
   19.1 events per member); DEC-15 holds at 22 of 22. What fails, at the default seed: (i) no genome is without a
   plasmid, and both plasmid types survive at only 14 of 22 seeds at the best rates found: rates that keep both
   types at every seed make every genome a carrier, so DEC-37 (3) and the spec's "0 to 2 plasmids per genome, some
   genomes without" exclude each other; (ii) N0's background genome sizes correlate with the signal world's at r
   0.81 (0.77 at a second seed) where the test asks 0.9: with one mild origination axis, size is no longer set by
   lineage, and no setting meets both DEC-37 (1) and that bound; (iii) the core genome collapsed: 7 root families
   are in at least 40 of 60 genomes, where the hub test asks 100 and the old world had 243; (iv) N1 finds no
   matched comparator family, from the same cause; (v) the intergenic burst identity is 0.895 against 0.9, and one
   RNA motif instance is missed at 27% divergence. The agent's reading: (iii) to (v) come from the default world
   being 1.95 times older than the one the turnover rates, the burst and the motif clades were fitted in (session
   2 fitted them at a crown of 2.56; DEC-37 (2) set "about the median", 5.26, on the orchestrator's recommendation,
   made before that was known). **The four questions**: (a) the background turnover at the fixed crown; (b)
   whether per-genome plasmid counts or survival at every seed gives way; (c) what replaces N0's r of 0.9; (d) the
   motif clade height and the 0.9 burst identity.
   **The orchestrator's first recommendation is withdrawn** (it was: fix the crown at the default seed's old age,
   2.56, so the rates keep their time unit). Measured at 20:30 (§11 of the note, branch `task/F-02-11` at
   `bb17e0e`): with the crown at 2.5632 and everything refitted, the core is 38 root families (bound 100), the
   burst identity 0.882 (lower, not higher), the motif instance still missed, N0's r 0.846; only N1's comparator
   comes back, and both plasmid types survive at 20 of 22 seeds instead of 14. The cause is elsewhere, shown by
   single-factor runs at the default seed: the old generator with its two lineage-rate axes gives a core of 243,
   with entry exclusion added 204, with no lineage axis 35, with the redesign's one origination axis 26 to 47. The
   old loss axis drifted most lineages to a loss multiplier well below 1, and a shared multiplier kept duplication
   and transfer low; DEC-37 (1) removed those axes and left the base rates where session 2 had set them for that
   world. (The agent later measured the old multipliers: it was the shared duplication and transfer multiplier
   that ran low, median 0.36 over lineages and 0.06 at the default seed; the loss multiplier's median was 0.85.)
   **Done without a decision** (the base rates are free preset values; branch at `89cfb46`, *measured* over 22
   seeds, §12 and §13 of the note): duplication, transfer and loss at 0.2 of session 2's values (0.008, 0.02,
   0.048), origination 0.065, member transfer 0.01, 0.03 and 0.4. The core is 253 root families at the default
   seed and 228 to 513 over the seeds (it was 7); the chromosome mean is 1,367 to 1,760, in band at 22 of 22; the
   planted share 4.4% to 6.5%; no exclusion breach; N1's comparator exists; the burst identity is 0.931 and the
   motif is found. That answers (a) and (d). **The session ended at about 01:15 on 2026-11-15** (branch at
   `5de6adc`; §14 of the note) with six assertions failing at the default seed and the finding that what is left
   is about four tests, not about rates. Its closest preset, **candidate B** (background duplication 0.012,
   transfer 0.045, loss 0.076, origination 0.035; not committed), passes everything at the default seed except
   two: the cross-phylum IGR pair count, 195 against 200, and N0's correlation; it keeps the mean in band and the
   core at 100 or more at 22 of 22 seeds (core 168 at the default seed), and with it the plasmid test passes at the
   default seed. **The four questions, each with a recommendation**:
   (1) *The motif test's premise.* It asserts that the planted instance is the best window at its coordinates.
   A tandem duplication of the gene that carries the motif puts a second copy of the same planted instance 257
   bases away with fewer substitutions, and the scan rightly prefers it (read from the events of two worlds; it
   happened in 4 of the 9 full worlds built, at any duplication rate above zero). Recommendation: the test
   accepts the best window when it is a planted instance of the same emission; that is a correction of the test,
   written into the next session's card as a migration.
   (2) *Decoys.* "At least 3 transfers per decoy" holds at 7 to 11 of 22 seeds at every setting tried: decoy
   transfer 0.4 against loss 0.4 is critical, and the partner modules are rare by design. It is a property of the
   preset, not a draw. Recommendation: raise the decoys' transfer rate until the bound holds at every seed (a
   refit, no change of the test), or, if you prefer the rates, ask it at the default seed only; say which.
   (3) *Plasmids* (b), over 22 seeds (*measured*, at the `89cfb46` preset): at rates 0.8 and 0.4 both types
   survive at 15 seeds, some genome is plasmid-free at 10, a genome has more than 2 at 4; at 0.1 and 0.3 both
   survive at 17, some genome is plasmid-free at 22 of 22, more than 2 at 2; at 3.0 and 0.1 both survive at 22 of
   22 and every genome carries both. Recommendation: 0.1 and 0.3, that is, keep the spec's per-genome range and
   replace DEC-37 (3)'s "at every seed" by "at the default seed, and reported per seed".
   (4) *N0's size check* (c), over 22 seeds (*measured*): the ratio of means is in [0.9, 1.1] at 22 of 22 (0.973
   to 1.091); r is at least 0.9 at 3 seeds; a two-sample KS test passes at only 11 (sizes are tight, so a shift
   of a few percent is a large KS distance). Recommendation: keep the ratio bound and drop the correlation, which
   measured the lineage axes that DEC-37 (1) removed.
   With your four answers the next session (a new agent) takes candidate B, nudges background transfer until the
   IGR count is at least 200 at the default seed (it follows transfer: 188 at 0.035, 195 to 200 at 0.045, 236 to
   262 at 0.05, *measured*), refits what moves, re-pins, and merges. Also seen and not explained: N1's label
   pairs are more adjacent than matched random pairs in 1 of 5 full null-world runs (p 0.0055 at the committed
   preset; it passes in both candidates), from pairs of relabelled background families that sit beside each
   other. Nothing of session 11 is merged.
6. **Which release holds the CRISPR arrays** (card C-33, stopped at its own stop condition at 22:19 on 2026-11-14,
   nothing written). The arrays themselves can go onto the seven built stages with the code as it is (new
   producer files; `rM.2` and `rN.3` keep verifying, since a release ignores stage files it does not list), and
   that part needs no decision. But a release cannot take them without a release of its own, and a release reads
   only its own `release=<R>` cluster files: a new `rM.3` over the same seven stages would cluster the same
   45,815,843 proteins again, about 14,087 CPU-minutes (*derived* from `rM.2`'s run records: 14,137.3 for its 449
   release records less 50.5 for tokens, postings, dimension tables, manifest and catalog; *measured*, wilkcomp02,
   2026-11-14), to produce bytes we already have twice. The same will happen for every later producer (the ncRNA
   of DEC-42) and every release that adds no protein. Options: **A**, `rM.3` with a re-clustering; **B**, finish
   `rM.1` after the arrays are on the stages: it is clustered, names the same seven stages, its cluster files are
   byte-identical to `rM.2`'s, and its remaining steps cost about 50.5 CPU-minutes, but the name would then sit
   after `rM.2`, and open item 1 would be reversed; **C**, a card that lets a new release take the clustering of
   an earlier one when the two cluster plans' input hashes are equal (the files copied or referenced into
   `release=<new>` with a ledger row, verified), so a release that adds no protein costs tokens, postings and a
   manifest only. **Recommendation: C**, as a card of tier M once you agree to the rule; it is the only option
   that also serves ncRNA and later producers, and it keeps release names in order. B is the cheap way to have
   arrays in a release this weekend if you want that first. Also found on the way, for a small card whatever you
   choose: the tokens step builds tokens with no cluster levels, without an error, when a release has no
   `cluster_member` files (`l3/tokens.py`, read in the code, not run). And a new release needs a pack of its own
   (the pack id covers the release id): about 403 CPU-minutes (*measured* for `rM.2`).
   **Update, 2026-11-15 07:10 (the orchestrator's plan, cheap to reverse until card C-35 starts)**: the next
   stages add proteins (H1, then PLSDB), so the release that takes them in must cluster again whatever is chosen
   here. Cards C-24 and C-26 are therefore amended to build their stages only, and one release over the nine
   stages follows (card C-35): one clustering run instead of two (about 14,650 CPU-minutes saved, *measured* for
   `rM.2`), and that release lists the arrays of all nine stages and the label files of all of them. So the
   arrays reach a release without option A or B. Option C stays open, for you: it is what ncRNA (DEC-42) and any
   later producer on unchanged proteins will need. If you would rather have a release per stage, say so before
   C-35 starts.
7. **How the H1 build resumes** (card C-24; the pod is idle on this since 08:54 on 2026-11-15). The agent's own
   driver stops the build when a step's measured CPU misses its estimate by more than 20% (AGENTS §6). It fired on
   Pfam scan shard 13, the plan's remainder shard of 1,347 residues: estimate 0.011 CPU-minutes (*derived*: 1,347
   x 7.96 per million), measured 1.533 (*measured*, its run record), which is the fixed cost of loading Pfam, so
   142.9 times its estimate. The nine full shards ran at 0.887 to 0.920 of their estimates, ingest at 0.865, gene
   calling at 0.936, the default CRISPR producer at 0.892 (*measured*). Stopping the unit killed shard 6 near its
   end and shards 9 to 12 at their start: about 240 CPU-minutes lost (*derived*). The stop was clean and the stage
   resumes from its published steps; about 1,080 CPU-minutes of scan remain (*derived*: 5 shards at about 215).
   The agent then tried to narrow its guard (judge only steps estimated at 24 CPU-minutes or more) and **the
   session's permission check refused that edit as weakening a stop condition**. A refused permission is not
   worked around, by the agent or by the orchestrator, so nothing was changed and the build was not resumed by
   another route. **Asked of you, one of**: (a) allow the guard to judge only steps estimated at 24 CPU-minutes
   or more (the agent's proposal; the orchestrator's recommendation: the rate rule is about rates, and a step of
   a hundredth of a CPU-minute has none; every step that can matter is far above the threshold); (b) resume with
   the guard as it is and shard 13's record acknowledged by hand, if you prefer no code change (it would fire
   again on any later remainder shard); (c) leave H1 as it is for now. Also from this build, not waiting on you:
   H1 has 1,608,023 distinct proteins where the card estimated 0.6 M (the scan is sized by the code from the real
   residues, 3,120 CPU-minutes in 14 shards); a release cannot be defined over a stage that is not ingested, so
   `rM.3` is named only by `--stages` until `PL1` exists (accepted; cards C-26 and C-35 name it the same way).

- Earlier the same day: DEC-29 (unsplittable clustering call as one process), W-25 approved, zombi2 approved for F-02.

Accepted as recommended on 2026-11-14 (kept for their reasons; none is open):
- Card F-02 (session 3): in the synthetic world a family's copies are found by `hmmsearch` equally often at every divergence
  (about 81%), because the spec's model keeps every copy a draw from the family's profile. Real families get harder to detect
  as they diverge. Should the model change so that detectability falls with divergence (a sharper root, an equilibrium that
  relaxes toward the background, or roots taken from real seed members: `Pfam-A.seed.gz` is on the pod)? Recommendation: yes,
  before the truth-table and null-world sessions: the statistics will be judged on families that annotation finds only
  partly, and a world where remote copies are as easy as close ones overstates what they can do. Also to confirm: the
  substitution, rate, clock and codon parameters the session chose (listed on the card).
- Card C-21b: a contig's genomes are looked up at one row group per stage file (19 at 20 stages, *measured*); a
  per-release copy of the membership sorted by contig would make it one. Recommendation: not yet, until a search
  needs it.
- Card W-22: the host's pack directory is the profile's `<tmp>/packs`; an explicit `packs:` key in the profile needs
  a change to `profile.py`. Recommendation: not needed now.
- Card E-08: two real features lose by Rfam's own clans (small SRP to large SRP in *B. subtilis*; GlmY to GlmZ in
  *E. coli*), and the greedy rule differs from `cmscan`'s only in chains of four or more overlapping hits.
  Recommendation: accept.
- Card F-02: the `micro` world's genome sizes: the spec's mean (about 1,500 genes) and range (1,000 to 4,000) cannot both
  hold; session 2 reached the range with a mean of 1,873. Recommendation: keep the range.
- Card F-02 (session 5): the Cas operon beside each CRISPR array is planted by the phage and CRISPR stage, not made as a
  ZOMBI2 group as the spec's §2.1 S2 lists, because a group in the host run would change every world and number of
  sessions 1 to 4. Cas genes are then inherited down their clade and never transferred. Recommendation: accept; a null
  world can also drop Cas loci without touching the backbone. Also to confirm: the phage, prophage and CRISPR parameters
  the session chose (listed on the card).
- Card F-02 (session 6): the `micro` world's 3-minute target will not hold. S1 to S7 take about 177 s on the loaded pod
  (*measured*: 36.6 + 78.7 + 45.9 + 10.6 + 5 to 6), and S8 to S11 are still to come; the session's estimate for the whole
  is 3 min 15 s to 3 min 30 s. The orchestrator has told the next session to apply S8's edits and S9's cuts in one
  rewrite of the genomes (fewer passes and one coordinate frame fewer), and not to thin the world. Recommendation: move
  the target to 4 minutes, measured on an idle machine. Also: should NOGD be allowed on profile-only and Cas module
  members, not only planted ones? Recommendation: planted only, as built; the truth tables stay simple. And the
  session's values to confirm (on the card).
- Card F-02 (session 7): S1 to S10 now take 195 to 209 s on the loaded pod (*derived*: sums of the stage times of three
  runs), with S11 still to come: the 4-minute target recommended above still fits. To confirm: (1) contig breaks go to
  every repeat up to the N50 budget (764 repeat breaks; in 200-kb drafts the repeats alone exceed the planned count, so
  a random subset is used); (2) contamination comes from the farthest genome of the same world, not from an outgroup
  world (all three contaminated hosts got the same donor); (3) near duplicates at 99.9% ANI are expected to collapse,
  as exact ones are; (4) the session's values (on the card). Recommendation: accept (1) and (3); for (2) accept for
  `micro` and ask for a second donor rule later, since one donor for all hosts is a thin test of a contamination screen.
- Card F-02 (session 8, `planning/measurements/2026-11-13_synthworld_emit.md`): (1) the `micro` world's build time is
  now whole: S1 to S11 take 227 to 232 s on the loaded pod (*measured* and *derived*), against the spec's 3 minutes.
  Recommendation: set the target to 5 minutes on the pod (the 4 recommended earlier would be met by 8 s). (2) Gene
  starts: on untouched genes the caller finds 99.4% by their stop but only 82.5% with the true start (*measured*). Real
  callers do better than that on real genomes, so either the world's start signals (ribosome-binding sites, start
  codon context) are weaker than nature's or the caller is being fooled by the planted upstream ORFs. Recommendation:
  a short measurement before the statistics use start-dependent features (the validation session, §7, compares the
  world with the golden genomes: put this first). (3) Truth tables as Parquet: the session proposes a registry and lock
  of their own, tables in the world directory keyed by the world digest, never in a data root. Recommendation: accept;
  truth is not release data. (4) The session's values (mislabel rate 0.05, six biomes, quality mapping; on the card).
- Card E-13 (`planning/measurements/2026-11-14_crispr_min_repeats.md`): which two-repeat CRISPR arrays to keep.
  Measured on P1 and P2 (10.6 Gbp): a minimum of 2 everywhere adds 31,413 calls to the 2,577 arrays found at 3, and
  almost all are false (42.7% lie wholly inside a gene, 40.2% are in genomes with no Cas gene; near a Cas gene they
  are only 1.4 to 1.6 times as common as random placement, against 33 times for the arrays found at 3). Kept only
  when within 1 kb of a Cas-domain gene, not inside a gene and not overlapping an array found at 3, they are 78
  (7.35 per Gbp, at most 2.96 per Gbp of them by chance, *derived* from random placement), and they include all 7
  Tn7-like loci of the worked example that gain an array (six 2-repeat arrays and one of 3 repeats, each 117 to 133
  bases from Cas6f), the control's among them. Recommendation: keep 3 everywhere and add that filtered second
  producer after gene calls and the Pfam scan (card E-15, to be written on your yes); never 2 everywhere. Also found:
  MinCED does not hold its own maximum spacer length (first spacers of up to 224 bases).
- Card E-12 (`planning/measurements/2026-11-13_crispr_arrays.md`), three questions (the first is answered by the
  item above). (1) MinCED's minimum of 3 repeats
  misses the very arrays the worked example is about: the Tn7-like element of the control has a 2-repeat, 1-spacer
  array 128 bases from Cas6f, found only with a minimum of 2 (*measured*). Two-repeat calls also arise by chance and in
  repeated genes. Recommendation: card E-13 measures the false-positive rate at 2 on the built stages (arrays far from
  any Cas gene, arrays inside genes) before the setting changes; a new setting is a new producer name, so nothing is
  rewritten. (2) Arrays are features and `l1_crispr` rows but not tape slots: `nbr` does not show them, and putting
  them on the tape would shift gene windows. Recommendation: keep them off the tape; show arrays beside a locus from
  `l1_crispr` by coordinates (card E-14, which also teaches the provenance check the producer's name). (3) When arrays
  enter a release: the step costs 182 CPU-seconds per Gbp (*derived* from stage P1), about 2.5 CPU-hours for all
  seven built stages (*derived*: 50.5 Gbp x 182 s). Recommendation: run it on the built stages after `rM.2` is
  verified and copied, as stage files of their own (no earlier file changes), for the release after `rM.2`.
- Card F-02 (session 10, `planning/measurements/2026-11-14_synthworld_seeds.md`), **blocks the statistics' calibration; the PI's design call**: the session measured the preset over 21 seeds and stopped without a fix, as briefed. Only 6 of 21 seeds have a chromosome mean in the spec's band (means 404 to 15,445), both plasmid types survive at 5, all 25 modules stay whole in 9 genomes at 1. Causes (*measured*, six seeds, one factor at a time): genome size is set by the two lineage-rate axes, not tree depth (axes off: means 1,548 to 1,825 at every seed, but then no seed reaches the 1,000 to 4,000 spread); the planted share comes from the module rate imbalance; plasmids decay with depth. No small change fixes all three and keeps the strength grid's transfer axis. Five questions are in the note's section 4; the orchestrator's recommendations: (1) ask per seed only for the mean within 30%, keep the spread as a default-seed property or drop it, and replace the two axes by one on origination; (2) cap module copies by entry exclusion and redefine the grid's transfer axis as realized transfers per member, refitted so the three levels differ; (3) fix the crown age, at about the 21-seed median; (4) refit plasmid rates over the 21 seeds; (5) measure the crown from the extant MRCA. Until decided, F-02 does not change the generator; the N2 worlds and F-03's calibration wait. Merged as a measurement (new files only; the generator and every digest unchanged).
- Card F-02 (session 9, `planning/measurements/2026-11-13_synthworld_nulls.md`), **important** (superseded in part by the session 10 item above): (1) the `micro` preset
  gives a full-size world only at its default seed. At seed 20261113 the signal world averages 701 genes per genome
  (1,937 at the default), about 70% of them planted families, and both plasmid types die out (*measured* and
  *derived*). Cause: module families lose copies at 0.03 against the genome's 0.24 and transfer at up to 0.30 against
  0.10, so over a longer tree (crown age about 8.3 against about 4) they take over; even at the default seed they are
  21.5% of the genes. The 20 calibration seeds (N2) and any rotating seed will hit this, so calibration cannot start
  until it is fixed. Recommendation: the next session measures size and planted share over the 20 seeds, then refits
  (a fixed crown age, and module rates balanced against the background) so every seed stays in the spec's range; this
  changes every stage digest once, and nothing downstream uses them yet. The orchestrator will start that session on
  this recommendation unless told otherwise, and merge it only with the before-and-after evidence. (2) N1 relabels
  only 27 or 28 of 100 module members under the spec's bound of 25% on transfer counts (47 to 52 evaluated pairs per
  world). Recommendation: keep the bound; it is what makes the relabel a fair null, and 20 worlds give about 1,000
  pairs. (3) The session's readings of §4.1 to confirm: decoys and NOGD leave both N0 worlds; N0-full keeps CRISPR-Cas
  loci and excludes pairs inside one locus from null evaluation, N0-strict drops the loci; chassis pairs are excluded,
  cargo pairs are traps; plasmid pairs are null. Recommendation: accept.
- Card B-42: add this decision row (the next DEC number; DEC-34 to DEC-37 were used on 2026-11-14)? "**DEC-25's one-process exemption for clustering has ended; DEC-29 is
  clustering's only exemption.** Clustering a release in one process is held to the 300 CPU-minute rule like every other
  step: above 80% of the budget `build-stage` clusters the release in parts, and clustering in one process estimated over
  `cpu_min_per_process` is refused whatever `cluster_cpu_min_max` says; that key now caps only a call that no number of parts
  fits (DEC-29). Basis: `rN.1b` reclustered in parts gave cluster files byte-identical to the published ones, every run
  record under 300 CPU-minutes (largest 92.7), *measured* 2026-11-12." The code already behaves so (card B-42, as amended
  under DEC-29). Recommendation: add it.
- Card W-28: (1) make the profile's durable directory, `/stor/work/Wilke/if2453/all-the-data` (it has never existed; our
  durable copies are under `/stor/scratch/Wilke/if2453/all-the-data/provisional/`), or say that results go under `bulk`
  (`/stor/scratch`) instead; (2) then the first real bundle is yours to write (the command is in the W-28 row's report:
  `results archive ... --with ...` then `results flush`; 164.6 MB for the RNA polymerase run); (3) may a run be archived
  again later with further outputs, as a second archive? Recommendation: results under `bulk` beside the other durable
  copies, and yes to (3).
- Scratch on the pod (item 24 of the table of 2026-11-14): may the 23,840 fetched candidate genomes not kept (about
  29.7 GB), the 13.6 GB of sketches, the local copy of a tar (11.2 GB) and the old test directories listed under "On
  the pod" be removed from `/tmp`? Recommendation: yes; the kept genomes are on `/stor`.
- `provisional/s2c_phase2/` on `/stor` has no `MANIFEST.sha256` (the two tars' hashes are in `sources.yaml`; a manifest
  file would be under 64 MB). Recommendation: leave it; `sources.yaml` is the record.
- Card W-31 (`planning/measurements/2026-11-13_flanks_context_types.md`): defaults of `nbr flanks`, three questions.
  (1) Context types: keep the fixed cut 0.5. The trees of mobile elements have no gap to cut at (merge distances spread
  evenly from 0.5 to 1), so the largest-gap rule made more types than 0.5 in three of four views (115 against 108 on the
  Cas7f loci); `--auto-cut N` is useful only on an ungrouped view (18 types at N = 10, none mixing complete type I-F,
  phage and Tn7-like loci). Recommendation: keep 0.5, keep `--auto-cut N`, remove `--auto-cut gap` before it is documented.
  (2) `--colour-fallback pfam` on by default at NR50 and NR90 when the release has a library: it colours 37 of the 39
  Cas genes of the Tn7-like loci, against 0 today. Recommendation: yes; and where one family would then show in two
  colours (its common cluster's and the Pfam colour of its rare clusters), give the whole family the Pfam colour.
  (3) `--colour-weight anchor-nr90` on by default, so 42 near-identical phage loci do not take every colour.
  Recommendation: yes. Taking (2) and (3) changes the bytes of every NR50 or NR90 figure (tables stay), and needs the
  guides and the CRISPR example updated in a card of their own.

AGENTS §4.3 says every output is byte-identical. Proposed wording for the PI to accept: "the catalog is generated from the manifest and is excluded from byte identity; its logical content is tested".
- Done 2026-11-11: card 0-08 (ncRNA) is decided by DEC-28.
- Card 0-07: a cluster's id is a hash of its member set, so it changes whenever the cluster gains a protein: 39.9% of NR50 clusters lost their id from `rL.2` to `rL.3` while 94.4% of proteins stayed with their old cluster's main fragment (*measured*). Recommendation: keep the id and add a lineage table that maps each cluster to its best-overlap predecessor.
- Card 0-05: two S5 storage budgets are exceeded (annotations 107 GB against 50; tapes plus postings 75 GB against 25 to 60), because every table now carries a 16-byte `feature_uid`. Recommendation: raise the budgets.
- Card 0-04: keep `token_postings` self-contained (about 36 GB at GTDB scale) or drop the columns the tape already holds (about a third smaller, at the price of a join). Recommendation: keep.
- `l0_manifest` is still one file rewritten per stage, against the append-only rule (found in B-15; no card yet: see B-22 in `TASKS.md`).
